Original Instructions

Introduction
The Introduction chapter contains general information about the
CS844, C844, and S844 Carbon/Sulfur Analyzers, including
specifications. Reference this chapter for replacement parts, operating
supplies, and optional accessories. To place an order by phone, call our
customer service department toll-free in the United States at
1-800-292-6141 or 269-985-5496. Orders may also be sent by fax to
269-982-8977.
The screenshot illustrations used throughout the manual are examples
only and may or may not be applicable to specific procedures.

1–18 844 Series Introduction

LECO-Supplied Balances
Sartorius® Support
All LECO-supplied Sartorius balances are supported and covered by
warranty through Sartorius.
The direct support and warranty coverage by Sartorius with LECO-
supplied Sartorius balances ensures that any balance-related service
issues are handled directly by the experts at Sartorius. This eliminates
the unnecessary step of working through the LECO service
professionals for a resolution from Sartorius.
For more information, access the Sartorius website by following the
link below and selecting the appropriate country:
https://www.sartorius.com/en/general-contact/local-contact
To expedite service, please have your Sartorius balance serial number
and model number available when contacting Sartorius.
Introduction 844 Series 1–19

About this Manual
The organization of this instruction manual is explained below. For
each chapter, there is a hyperlinked table of contents and list of
illustrations, if applicable.
The screenshots used throughout the manual are examples only and
may or may not be applicable to specific procedures.
Chapter 1, Introduction, page 1–3, describes general information
including specifications. This chapter also provides information about
parts and accessories.
Chapter 2, Installation, page 2–1, describes hardware setup and
connection.
Chapter 3, Analysis, page 3–1, describes how to analyze samples.
Chapter 4, Settings, page 4–1, describes how to configure parameters
in the software.
Chapter 5, Instrument, page 5–1, describes how to configure the
instrument.
Chapter 6, Maintenance, page 6–1, describes procedures to perform
on a regular basis to improve the instrument’s performance and
lifespan.
Chapter 7, Theory of Operation, page 7–1, describes the theory of
operation.
Chapter 8, Diagnostics, page 8–1, describes how to monitor the
instrument to ensure proper operation.
Chapter 9, Service, page 9–1, describes service procedures. Contact
the LECO Service Department for further information.
Chapter 10, Illustrations, page 10–1, provides illustrations and
photographs that can assist with procedures and location of parts.
Chapter 11, Schematics, page 11–1, provides diagrams of circuit
boards and wiring.
Chapter 12, Glossary, page 12–1, provides definitions of terms.
Chapter 13, Index, page 13–1, provides page numbers for topics
throughout the manual. In the electronic manual available through the
software, the page numbers in the index provide a hyperlink to the
corresponding topic.

Specifications
Instrument Range*
Carbon**............................................0.0006 to 60 mg
Sulfur**.............................................Standard Mode: 0.0006 to 60 mg
ES Mode: 0.0001 to 0.05 mg
Precision†
Carbon:..............................................0.0003 mg or 0.5% RSD, whichever is
greater
Sulfur:...............................................Standard Mode:0.0003 mg or 0.5% RSD,
whichever is greater
ES Mode: 0.00005 mg or 0.5% RSD,
whichever is greater
Calibration ..........................................Standards (single or multipoint); manual;
gas dose
Analysis Time§....................................Standard Mode: 40 seconds
ES Mode: 130 seconds
Cycle Time§ (including purge, analysis delay, and analysis time)
Standard Mode: 130 seconds
ES Mode: 270 seconds
Throughput.........................................Standard Mode: 27 samples per hour
(nominal)
ES Mode: 13 samples per hour (nominal)
Sample Size.........................................1 gram (nominal)
Detection Method................................Non-Dispersive Infrared Absorption
Chemical Reagents
Anhydrous Magnesium Perchlorate (MgClO4)
Sodium Hydroxide on an Inert Base
Platinized Silica Gel
Rare Earth Copper Oxide
Cellulose
Gas Requirements
Carrier ...............................................Oxygen, 99.5% pure, 35 psi (2.41 bar)
±10%
Pneumatic ..........................................Compressed Air (oil, water free), 40 psi
(2.76 bar) ±10%
Dosing: CS/C844.................................Carbon Dioxide, 99.99% pure, 20 psi
(1.38 bar) ±10%
1–40 844 Series Introduction

Gas Flow Rates
Carrier ...............................................Standard Mode: 3 L/minute
ES Mode: 0.8 L/min
Pneumatic ..........................................1 L/analysis
Furnace...............................................Induction, 2.2 kW max (rampable 0 to
100% power), liquid cooled
Coolant................................................300 mL LECO Coolant
Operating Conditions
Operating Temp ..................................15 to 35 °C (59 to 95 °F)
Rel. Humidity......................................20 to 80% (non-condensing)
Sound Pressure Level..........................62 dbB excluding vacuum (max reading at
operator’s level per IEC/EN 61010-1)
Physical Dimensions††........................33 in H x 26 in W x 30 in D (84 cm x
64 cm x 75 cm) with touchscreen monitor
Electrical Power Requirements............230 V~ (+10%/-15% at Max Load);
50/60 Hz, single phase, 25 A;
5,500 Btu/hr ‡
Weight (approx.).................................308 lb (140 kg) with monitor
292 lb (132 kg) without monitor
*Use the following formula to calculate element composition:
% element composition = ((absolute element mass in mg) / (sample mass in
mg)) *100
**Lower range is calculated as 2 σ instrument blank deviation. Method range may
differ due to factors such as sample type and method parameters.
†Calculated as 1 σ instrument blank deviation. Method precision may differ due to
sample inhomogeneity or other external factors.
§All times listed are nominal, actual times may vary based on method settings and
application.
††Allow for a 6-inch (15 cm) minimum access area around sides.
‡
Average output based on nominal operating parameters.

page 2–12, for the location of the ON/OFF switch.
PROTECTIVE EYEWEAR/PROTECTIVE GLOVES
Protective eyewear and gloves should be worn when handling
chemicals.
Refer to the Safety Data Sheet (SDS) for the specific chemical
for additional information.

Lifting and Moving the Instrument
CAUTION
Refer to Specifications, page 1–40, for the approximate
shipping and instrument weight. To lift or move the
instrument, use equipment capable of safely lifting this
weight.
1. Properly distribute weight prior to lifting.
CAUTION
Front panels and other cosmetic parts of this instrument are
not designed to be weight bearing. DO NOT use such parts as
lifting points, or damage may result. ALWAYS lift near the feet
at the sides or the rear of the instrument base.
2. Position the instrument in a location convenient to power and gas
supplies, providing a minimum access space of 6 inches
(15.2 cm) around the instrument. The location for the instrument
must also be adequately rated to support the weight of the
instrument and all accessories.
3. Visually inspect the instrument for damage.

Installing the Analyzer
CAUTION
Many substances that do not normally burn in air, as well as
other substances that are combustible in oxygen, may burn
violently when a higher percentage of oxygen is present.
OXIDIZING/PROTECTIVE EYEWEAR/PROTECTIVE
GLOVES
Oxygen is an oxidizer and is pressurized. It can become
volatile when subjected to heat or high temperatures.
Protective eyewear and gloves should be worn when handling
oxygen.
Refer to the Safety Data Sheet (SDS) for additional
information.
· Follow all local regulations for storage and handling of oxygen.
Keep combustibles away from oxygen and eliminate ignition
sources.
· Do not allow oil, grease, or other readily combustible materials
to come in contact with a cylinder or equipment used for
oxidant services. Use only equipment that is intended for
oxygen service. Use only a regulator that is designated and
labeled “cleaned for oxygen service.”
· Do not use plastic tubing to supply oxygen to the analyzer.
Copper/copper-base or nickel/nickel-base alloys are preferred
for oxygen supply lines because the ignition temperature and
burn resistance is higher than plastic.
· Oxygen, as a liquid or cold gas, may cause severe frostbite to
the skin or eyes. Do not touch frosted pipes or valves.
· For more detailed information, consult the Safety Data Sheet
(SDS) provided by your oxygen supplier and review local
regulations and guidelines for oxygen piping.
The following procedure should be used for first-time installation of the
instrument. This procedure assumes that the instrument has been
unpacked and positioned in its permanent location. Refer to Lifting and
Moving the Instrument, and 2–5.
The balance and printer must be purchased separately; they are not
supplied with the instrument.
For power and gas supply requirements, refer to Specifications,
page 1–40.
Installation 844 Series 2–13

CAUTION
For proper operation, the analyzer must be installed on a
sturdy and level surface.
HAZARDOUS VOLTAGE WARNING
During installation and operation of this instrument, the
On/Off switch must be easily accessible. Refer to Figure 2-4,
page 2–12, for the location of the ON/OFF switch.
If a dual-instrument configuration is being used:
· Ensure enough space is provided for both instruments and the
monitor when finding a suitable location.
· Ensure that a Cornerstone® brand software registration key that
contains configurations for each instrument is used.
1. Provide a work surface with convenient electrical power and gas
supplies. Refer to Specifications, page 1–40.
2. Install the computer near the instrument. Do not turn On the
computer until instructed to do so in step 15.D, page 2–16. Refer
to Installing the Computer, page 2–24.
3. OPTIONAL: Install the balance near the computer and
instrument by completing the steps in Installing the Balance
(Optional), page 5–13. A balance must be purchased separately
and is optional equipment.
4. Connect the oxygen tubing assembly from the oxygen tank to
Oxygen In on the rear of the instrument. Refer to Instrument
Rear Panel Overview, page 2–10.
The oxygen inlet fitting is an adapter that accommodates the 1/8-inch
copper tubing supplied in the component pack. If 1/4-inch tubing is
preferred, remove the adapter and connect the 1/4-inch tubing (not
supplied in the component pack).
5. Turn On the oxygen gas at the tank, and then set the regulator to
35 psi (2.4 bar).
6. If required, connect the carrier flow exhaust, on the rear of the
instrument, to the facility exhaust. Refer to Instrument Rear
Panel Overview, page 2–10.
7. Connect the pneumatic tubing assembly by completing the
following steps.
A. Connect the pneumatic tubing assembly from the pneumatic
tank to the pneumatic fitting on the rear of the instrument.
Refer to Instrument Rear Panel Overview, page 2–10.
B. Push the tubing into the pneumatic fitting until it is seated.
Pull back on the tubing to make sure it is locked in place. If it
is necessary to remove the tubing from the instrument, push
in on the outer ring of the fitting while pulling the tubing out.
C. Turn On the pneumatic gas at the tank and set the regulator
to 40 psi (2.8 bar).
2–14 844 Series Installation

CAUTION/PROTECTIVE EYEWEAR/PROTECTIVE
GLOVES
Carbon dioxide is a gas under pressure. It can become volatile
when subjected to heat or high temperatures. It is an
asphyxiant and may displace the oxygen in the room.
Protective gloves and eyewear should be worn at all times
when handling this material.
Refer to the Safety Data Sheet (SDS) for additional
information.
8. Connect the gas dose tubing assembly from the gas dose tank to
Dose A In and Dose B In on the rear of the instrument. A “T”
assembly is provided to connect both inlets if desired. Refer to
Instrument Rear Panel Overview, page 2–10.
A. Turn On the dose gas at the tank, and then set the regulator
to 20 psi (1.4 bar).
B. If required, connect the Dose A Exhaust, Dose B Exhaust, and
pressure monitor, on the rear of the instrument, to the facility
exhaust.
The PRESSURE MONITOR should be used whenever the DOSE A
EXHAUST and DOSE B EXHAUST outlets are routed to an external vent.
This ensures that the pressure measured at the PRESSURE MONITOR
port is the same as the DOSE A EXHAUST and the DOSE B EXHAUST.
9. Install the oscillator tube and fill the coolant reservoir. Refer to
Installing the Oscillator Tube, page 2–18.
10. Install the bracket for the vacuum cleaner.
11. Install the vacuum cleaner. Refer to Installing the Vacuum
Cleaner, page 2–22.
12. Remove and pack the following reagent tubes according to the
procedures described in the appropriate maintenance section.
· Catalyst Tube (heated), page 6–17: (CS/C844)
· Cellulose Tube (nonheated), page 6–20: (CS/C844)
· Incoming Purifier Tube (heated), page 6–13: (CS/C844)
· Incoming Scrubber Tube (nonheated), page 6–9: (CS/C844)
· Flow Scrubber Tube (nonheated), page 6–22: (CS/C/S844)
13. Reinstall the reagent tube(s) if not already completed.
14. For installing a dual-instrument configuration only:
Install the 832 Series instrument. Refer to the 832 Series
Instruction Manual that came with your instrument.
15. Power on the instrument by completing the following steps.
A. Plug the instrument into facility AC power. Refer to Installing the
Electrical, page 2–20.
B. Turn On the power switch located on the left-side of the
instrument. Allow 1 to 2 minutes for the instrument to start.
C. Turn On the monitor.
Installation 844 Series 2–15

D. Turn On the computer. If using a non-LECO PC, refer to Setting
Up PC for Use, page 2–43.
E. When prompted, log in using the Customer account.
F. Use the control panel to confirm that the display settings are
configured to match the native resolution of the monitor and the
desired display orientation. Rotated in the portrait (flipped)
orientation is the default for most configurations.
G. Instrument software is installed during the manufacturing process
except when field installations are required. Install the software if
necessary. Refer to LECO Cornerstone Brand Software,
page 2–44.
H. Double-click the instrument software desktop icon to start the
software.
If a dual-instrument configuration is being used, launch the

software and allow for control of each instrument. Select the desired
|     | instance of Cornerstone | by selecting it from the Microsoft® |     |     |     |

Windows®
taskbar.
16. OPTIONAL: If a balance is installed, complete the balance setup.
Refer to Setting Up the Balance (Optional), page 5–14.

19. Install the pedestal base and pedestal. Refer to Installing the
Pedestal, page 2–19.

24. When the heaters have stabilized, perform a System Check by
completing the following steps.
A. In the software, select Diagnostics, and then select System
Check.
B. Select Start, and then wait for the System Check sequence to
finish.
C. Address any items that do not pass.
25. Perform a Leak Check to make sure the system is sealed and
does not leak. Refer to Leak Check, page 8–17.
26. Turn Off the carrier gas.
27. Instrument installation is now complete.
Installation 844 Series 2–17

Installing the Oscillator Tube
The procedure described in this section is only for the initial
installation of the oscillator tube and should be performed only by
LECO Service.
HAZARDOUS VOLTAGE WARNING
When the instrument is plugged in and power turned On, the
oscillator and the furnace power supply may contain high
voltage. Before proceeding with this procedure, always ensure
that the power switch (refer to Figure 2-4, page 2–12), is Off
and that the power cable is unplugged from facility power.
Use caution and the proper high voltage safety procedures
when working on and around these assemblies.
For illustrations of the oscillator assembly, refer to Figure 10-82, page
10–89, through Figure 10-90, page 10–97.
1. Turn Off the power switch (refer to Figure 2-4, page 2–12), and
unplug the instrument from the power source.
2. Remove the right side panel of the instrument.
3. Remove the bottom RF enclosure door and then the top RF
enclosure door to expose the oscillator chassis.
4. Install the new oscillator tube into the oscillator assembly by
pushing it into the socket. The socket is keyed, and the tube will
only go in one way.
5. Secure the plate connector to the top of the oscillator tube.
6. Fill the coolant reservoir by completing the following steps.
A. Remove the left side panel.
B. Remove the reservoir cap to the coolant reservoir, located at
the rear of the instrument. Refer to Figure 6-14, page 6–49.
C. Fill the coolant reservoir with the specified coolant until the
coolant level is approximately 1/2-inch from the top of the
reservoir and below the threads.
D. Reinstall the reservoir cap.
E. Inspect the clear tubing and fittings in the oscillator housing
on the right side of the instrument for leaks.
F. Inspect the blue tubing and fittings within the left side of the
instrument for leaks.
G. Inspect the fittings on the rear of the unit for leaks.
H. Reinstall the left side panel.
7. Reinstall the bottom and top RF enclosure doors, and tighten all
screws.
8. Reinstall the right side panel.
2–18 844 Series Installation

Installing the Pedestal
1. Install the dust cup filter on the lower seal assembly.
2. Install the pedestal base.
3. Install the pedestal on the pedestal base.
Figure 2-5
Pedestal Assembly

Installing the Vacuum Cleaner
The necessary equipment and components to install the vacuum
cleaner are located in the Component Pack.
1. Unpack the vacuum cleaner and open the filter compartment to
verify the vacuum filter is installed properly.
2. Set the vacuum power switch to On.
3. Plug the vacuum cleaner into the vacuum cleaner AC outlet on
the rear of the instrument. Refer to Instrument Rear Panel
Overview, page 2–10, for the location of the vacuum cleaner AC
outlet.
4. Locate the vacuum manifold assembly. Insert one o-ring on each
end of the block.
5. Attach one end of the hose supplied with the vacuum cleaner to
the vacuum manifold port that points away from the instrument.
Attach the other end of the hose to the vacuum cleaner.
6. Remove the three outermost screws beneath the stainless steel
work surface.
7. Remove the lower right casting.
2–22 844 Series Installation

8. Route the flexible silicone tubing between the vacuum manifold and
the left side of the tube union located on the front of the pedestal
cylinder. Secure the flexible silicone tubing using hose clamps.
The color of the flexible silicone tubing may vary.
9. Attach one end of the second vacuum hose to the open port on the
vacuum manifold. Route the hose beneath the instrument and attach
the remaining end to the hose block located on the mechanical flow
controller access panel inside the left door.
10. Reinstall the lower right casting and stainless steel work surface.
11. Slide the vacuum manifold into the bracket on the rear of the
unit.
Installation 844 Series 2–23

Installing the Computer
The computer system that consists of the computer, monitor, and
printer (optional) can be purchased from LECO or supplied by the user.
If the computer system is supplied by LECO, refer to Computer System
Supplied by LECO, following. If the user supplies the computer system,
refer to Computer System Supplied by the User, page 2–25.
Computer System Supplied by LECO
1. Unpack the computer.
2. Set the computer near the instrument.
3. Locate the keyboard, supplied with the computer, and set it in
front of where the monitor will be installed. Connect the keyboard
cable to a USB port on the rear of the computer.
4. Locate the mouse, supplied with the computer, and set it next to
the keyboard. Connect the mouse cable to a USB port on the rear
of the computer.
CAUTION: NETWORK CONNECTION
Connect the Ethernet port on the instrument to the
appropriate port on the computer as instructed in this manual.
Connecting the instrument directly to a corporate network
(LAN) may result in communication problems.
5. Locate the Ethernet cable and connect one end to the Ethernet
adapter card on the rear of the computer. Connect the other end
to the Ethernet Port on the rear of the instrument. Refer to
Instrument Rear Panel Overview, page 2–10.
6. Locate the Registration Key. Connect the key to a USB port on
the rear of the computer.
7. Install the monitor by completing the following steps.
· For systems with touchscreen monitors, refer to Analyzer-
Mounted Touchscreen Monitors, page 2–26, or Desktop-
Mounted Touchscreen Monitors, page 2–35.
· For systems with non-touchscreen desktop-mounted monitors,
complete the following steps:
1) Connect the monitor to a suitable AC power source. Refer
to the manual supplied with the monitor.
2) Connect the video cable, supplied with the monitor, to the
monitor and the computer. Refer to the manual supplied
with the desktop monitor.
2–24 844 Series Installation

8. OPTIONAL: Install the printer according to the manual provided
by the manufacturer.
9. Connect the computer's electrical supply connector to a suitable
AC power source. If the instrument and (optional) balance
installation is complete, turn On power to the computer. If the
installation is not complete, do not turn On power to the
computer until instructed to do so in Installing the Analyzer,
page 2–13.
Computer System Supplied by the User
If the Computer System is not supplied by LECO, the computer must
meet minimum requirements. Contact LECO for more information.
Installation 844 Series 2–25

Installing the Touchscreen Monitor
Depending on your instrument and monitor configuration, the
figures in the following sections may differ slightly from your setup.
Analyzer-Mounted Touchscreen Monitors
CAUTION
For instruments with connectors on the back panel only: To
prevent damage to the device, use only the power supply that
was included with the touchscreen monitor.
1. Unpack the monitor.
2. Complete the appropriate set of steps as follows, depending on
the instrument configuration, to route the cable assembly from
the monitor mounting point and out the back of the instrument.
· Monitors Mounted in the Middle of the Instrument Door,
following.
· Monitors Mounted to the Instrument Frame, page 2–29.
Monitors Mounted in the Middle of the Instrument Door:
A. Open the instrument door.
B. Using a Phillips screwdriver, remove the six screws that
secure the L-bracket onto the inside of the instrument door,
and then remove the L-bracket. Refer to Figure 2-7, following.
Figure 2-7
Cable Assembly Routed Through Instrument
2–26 844 Series Installation

C. From the front of the instrument door, use an Allen® wrench
(hex wrench) to loosen the setscrew underneath the monitor
arm bracket, until the monitor arm can be lifted off the
bracket. Set the monitor aside. Refer to Figure 2-8, following.
The setscrew can be viewed from behind the instrument door,
through the cable assembly hole, to aid in gauging when the
setscrew is loose enough. Refer to Figure 2-7, page 2–26, for the
location of the cable assembly hole.
Figure 2-8
Monitor Arm Bracket Setscrew
D. Route the cable assembly through the hole in the front of the
instrument door. Refer to Figure 2-8, previous. Make sure to
leave just enough cable on the outside of the instrument that
the monitor connections can be made.
E. Return the monitor arm to the bracket, and tighten the
setscrew to secure it in place.
F. Use the cable assembly clip to secure the cable assembly to
the monitor arm. Refer to Figure 2-8, previous.
G. 836, 736, 844, and 744 Series Instruments Only: The
cable assembly runs through a channel underneath the
instrument. Complete the following steps to prepare that
channel.
1) Remove the instrument side panel (left side when facing
the front of the instrument).
Installation 844 Series 2–27

2) Use a 9/64-inch Allen wrench to loosen and then remove
the screws that secure the cable assembly channel to the
underside of the instrument, and lower the channel to the
tabletop directly below the instrument. Refer to Figure
2-9, following.
Figure 2-9
Cable Assembly Channel Screws
H. From behind the instrument door, route the cable assembly
through the hole in the instrument bottom. Refer to Figure
2-7, page 2–26.
I. Push the length of cable assembly left outside the instrument
against the inside wall of the instrument door, between the
walls of the L-bracket channel. Refer to Figure 2-7,
page 2–26.
J. Hold the L-bracket in place and make sure the screw holes in
the bracket can align flush against the screw holes in the
instrument door without pinching the cable. If necessary,
readjust the cable.
K. Use the six screw removed in step B, page 2–26, to secure
the L-bracket to the inside of the instrument door.
L. Route the cable assembly underneath the instrument and out
the rear.
· In the 836, 736, 844, and 744 series instruments only, lay
the length of cable assembly inside the channel and then
out the rear through the hole in the instrument frame.
M. 836, 736, 844, and 744 Series Instruments Only:
Complete the following steps to secure the cable assembly
channel.
1) Lift up the channel and align the screw holes in the
channel with the screw holes in the instrument floor,
making sure the cable is not pinched.
2) While pushing up on the channel from underneath to hold
it in place, reinstall the screws previously removed in
step G.2), page 2–28.
3) Reinstall the side panel.
2–28 844 Series Installation

N. Close the instrument door.
O. Skip to step 3, page 2–30.
Monitors Mounted to the Instrument Frame:
Refer to Figure 2-10, page 2–29, while completing the following steps.
A. Use an Allen wrench (hex wrench) to loosen the setscrew
underneath the monitor arm bracket, until the monitor arm
can be lifted off the bracket. Set the monitor aside.
B. Route the cable assembly through the hole in the front of the
instrument frame. Make sure to leave just enough cable on
the outside of the frame that the monitor connections can be
made.
C. Return the monitor arm to the bracket, and tighten the
setscrew to secure it in place.
D. Use the cable assembly clip to secure the cable assembly to
the monitor arm.
E. Route the cable assembly as follows:
1) Down along the inside of the instrument frame and out the
hole in the bottom of the instrument.
2) Underneath the instrument and out the rear.
Figure 2-10
Cable Assembly Routed Through Instrument Frame
Installation 844 Series 2–29

3. Install the monitor by completing the following steps. In these
instructions, the monitors will be differentiated by the color of
their back covers: gray (Figure 2-11) or black (Figure 2-12).
Refer to the appropriate figure, depending on the monitor
received with the instrument, while completing the following
steps.
The spring-loaded stop pin can be positioned toward the top or
bottom of the analyzer as desired. Refer to Figure 2-12, page 2–31,
and Figure 2-11, following, for an example of each position.
A. Using a 9/64-inch Allen wrench (hex wrench), remove the
bottom stop screw.
B. Pull the stop pin in the mounting arm carriage to unlock.
Continue holding onto the stop pin until the monitor is in
position.
C. Slide the monitor carriage block onto the monitor rail on the
back of the monitor.
D. Release the stop pin into the desired hole alongside the
monitor rail to temporarily lock the monitor into position.
E. Reinstall the bottom stop screw to prevent the monitor from
falling out of the mount.
Figure 2-11
Gray Back Monitor Installation
2–30 844 Series Installation

Figure 2-12
Black Back Monitor Installation
| Installation | 844 Series | 2–31 |
| ------------ | ---------- | ---- |

4. Complete the appropriate set of steps as follows, depending on
the monitor received with your instrument, to connect the cable
assembly.
· Gray Back Monitor, following.
· Black Back Monitor, page 2–33.
Gray Back Monitor:
A. Use a #2 Phillips screwdriver to remove the three screws that
secure the cable access panel on the back of the monitor.
Refer to Figure 2-25, page 2–42.
B. Route the cable assembly in through the cable opening in the
monitor and out through the cable access panel opening as
shown in Figure 2-13, following. Pull just enough wire through
the opening to make the connections in step C, following.
C. Connect each of the cables into the monitor: power, video,
and USB.
Figure 2-13
Gray Back Monitor Cable Installation
D. Using the three screws removed in step A, previous, reinstall
the cable access panel on the monitor. Refer to Figure 2-25,
page 2–42.
E. Skip to step 5, page 2–34.
2–32 844 Series Installation

Black Back Monitor:
A. Connect each of the cables in the cable assembly into the
monitor: power, USB, video. Refer to Figure 2-14,
following.
B. Secure the cables to the loop saddles in the strain relief plate
with the included cable ties. Refer to Figure 2-14, following,
for the location of the cables and loop saddles.
Figure 2-14
Black Back Monitor Cable Installation
C. Remove the cable connection cover plate and cover plate
screws from the monitor packaging.
D. Trim the cable ties enough that the cable connection cover
plate can be installed.
Installation 844 Series 2–33

E. Using a #2 Phillips screwdriver, install the cable connection
cover plate with the included screw. Refer to Figure 2-15,
following.
Figure 2-15
Cable Connection Cover Plate
F. Using a 9/64-inch Allen wrench (hex wrench), loosen and
remove the two screws securing the cable support bracket to
the back of the monitor, and remove the bracket. Refer to
Figure 2-12, page 2–31, for the location of the cable support
bracket.
G. Reinstall the cable support bracket over the cables so that it
holds them in place. Refer to Figure 2-12, page 2–31.
5. Adjust the monitor to a comfortable viewing height and angle by
pulling out the stop pin in the mounting arm carriage. With the
stop pin pulled out, raise or lower the monitor to the appropriate
height. Release the stop pin into the desired hole alongside the
monitor rail to lock the monitor into position. Refer to Figure
2-11, page 2–30, (for the gray back monitor) or Figure 2-12,
page 2–31 (for the black back monitor).
6. Connect the USB cable, supplied with the monitor, to a USB port
on the rear of the computer.
2–34 844 Series Installation

Desktop-Mounted Touchscreen Monitors
1. Unpack the monitor.
2. Place the post for the desktop monitor stand into the base with
the groove toward the back of the base (in line with the screw
hole). Refer to Figure 2-16, following.
Figure 2-16
Monitor Post and Base
3. Using the 7/32-inch Allen wrench (hex wrench), tighten the
setscrew in the base to secure the post.
4. Install the post collar onto the tilt mechanism. Secure the two
pieces together using the black washer (with prongs) and the
black Phillips screw. Refer to Figure 2-17, following.
Make sure the prongs on the washer are not visible when the
washer is installed.
To increase or decrease the force required to tilt the monitor, adjust
the setscrew in the tilt mechanism. Refer to Figure 2-17, following.
Figure 2-17
Post Collar/Tilt Mechanism Assembly
Installation 844 Series 2–35

5. Attach the mounting plate to the tilt mechanism with the four
10-32 3/8-inch Phillips head screws supplied with the kit. Refer to
Figure 2-18, following.
Figure 2-18
Attach Mounting Plate
6. Install the tilt mechanism mounting plate to the monitor carriage
block by completing the following steps.
· To install the monitor with a vertical orientation:
1) Position the tilt mechanism mounting plate onto the
monitor carriage block as shown in Figure 2-19, following.
Figure 2-19
Vertical Monitor Installation
2) Secure the carriage block to the tilt mechanism mounting
plate with three Phillips screws, leaving the upper left
screw hole empty. Refer to Figure 2-19, previous, for
screw placement.
3) Install the thumbscrew in the fourth screw hole as a
carriage lock. Refer to Figure 2-19, previous.
2–36 844 Series Installation

· To install the monitor with a horizontal orientation:
1) Position the tilt mechanism mounting plate onto the
monitor carriage block as shown in Figure 2-20, following.
Figure 2-20
Horizontal Monitor Installation
2) Secure the carriage block to the tilt mechanism mounting
plate with three Phillips screws, leaving the upper left
screw hole empty. Refer to Figure 2-20, previous, for
screw placement.
3) Install the thumbscrew in the fourth screw hole as a
carriage lock. Refer to Figure 2-20, previous.
7. Place the monitor face down on a flat work surface or countertop.
8. Using a 9/64-inch Allen (hex) wrench, remove the bottom stop
screw from the back of the monitor. Refer to Figure 2-11, page
2–30, or Figure 2-12, page 2–31, for the location of the bottom
stop screw.
9. Slide the monitor carriage block onto the monitor rail on the back
of the monitor. Refer to Figure 2-11, page 2–30, or Figure 2-12,
page 2–31, for the location of the monitor carriage block and rail.
10. Reinstall the bottom stop screw.
Installation 844 Series 2–37

11. Complete the appropriate step, as follows, depending on the
monitor received with the instrument, to connect the cable
assembly. In these instructions, they will be differentiated by the
color of their back covers: gray or black.
· Gray Back Monitor, following.
· Black Back Monitor, page 2–39.
Gray Back Monitor:
A. Use a Phillips screwdriver to remove the three screws that
secure the cable access panel on the back of the monitor.
Refer to Figure 2-25, page 2–42.
B. Route the cable assembly in through the cable opening in the
monitor and out through the cable access panel opening as
shown in Figure 2-21, following. Pull just enough wire through
the opening to make the connections in step C, following.
C. Connect each of the cables into the monitor: power, video,
and USB.
Figure 2-21
Gray Back Monitor Cable Installation
D. Using the three screws removed in step A, previous, reinstall
the cable access panel on the monitor. Refer to Figure 2-25,
page 2–42.
E. Skip to step 12, page 2–41.
2–38 844 Series Installation

Black Back Monitor:
A. Connect each of the cables in the cable assembly into the
monitor: power, USB, video. Refer to Figure 2-22, following.
B. Secure the cables to the loop saddles in the strain relief plate
with the included cable ties. Refer to Figure 2-22, following,
for the location of the cables and loop saddles.
Figure 2-22
Black Back Monitor Cable Installation
C. Remove the cable connection cover plate and cover plate
screws from the monitor packaging.
D. Trim the cable ties enough that the cable connection cover
plate can be installed.
Installation 844 Series 2–39

E. Using a Phillips screwdriver, install the cable connection cover
plate with the included screw. Refer to Figure 2-23, following.
Figure 2-23
Cable Connection Cover Plate
F. Using a 9/64-inch Allen wrench (hex wrench), loosen and
remove the two screws securing the cable support bracket to
the back of the monitor, and remove the bracket. Refer to
Figure 2-12, page 2–31, for the location of the cable support
bracket.
G. Reinstall the cable support bracket over the cables so that it
holds them in place. Refer to Figure 2-12, page 2–31.
2–40 844 Series Installation

12. Carefully pick up the monitor assembly, and then slide it onto the
base assembly post. Tighten the wingnut to secure the monitor
into position. Refer to Figure 2-24, following.
The following figure shows the gray back monitor. Both monitors are
installed into the base assembly post in the same way.
Figure 2-24
Monitor Assembly
Installation 844 Series 2–41

13. Connect the power supply to the cable assembly.
14. Tighten the thumbscrew installed in step 6, page 2–36, to
prevent the monitor from wobbling.
Figure 2-25
Gray Back Monitor Rear Panel Installation
2–42 844 Series Installation

Setting Up PC for Use
· The following section applies only when using a non-LECO PC.
· Due to setup and hardware variations between computer
manufacturers, LECO cannot accommodate all possible
variables required for optimal operation of the Cornerstone
brand software and the LECO analyzer. For non-LECO
computers, contact the computer manufacturer’s service
department for support.
1. Install any required cards.
2. Connect keyboard, mouse, monitor, and power cords.
3. Turn On the computer tower.
4. Install and/or set up Microsoft® Windows® 10 64-bit operating
system or later.
5. If the computer connects to the instrument via an Ethernet
adapter, refer to the appropriate instructions in Instrument
Ethernet Adapter, page 2–45.
Windows Firewall may ask to allow communications to the
Cornerstone brand software. If prompted, these communications are
trustworthy and should be allowed.
Installation 844 Series 2–43

LECO Cornerstone Brand Software
LECO recommends performing the following procedures BEFORE
installing any new version of the Cornerstone brand software. This
will help identify any underlying hardware issues to be addressed
before software installation.
System Check, page 8–20.
Leak Check, page 8–17.
Validating the Database, page 5–24.
The following section includes the following procedures:
Required Settings for LECO Software, following.
Instrument Ethernet Adapter, page 2–45.
Installing the Software, page 2–46
Configuring Firewall Settings, page 2–48.
Troubleshooting Registration Key, page 2–48.
Uninstalling LECO Software, page 2–49.
Required Settings for LECO Software
Windows 10 (64-bit) or later is required for LECO Cornerstone brand
software. Make sure that all the latest Windows updates have been
installed.
Operating System (OS) User Permission Requirements
The following table shows the minimum permissions that a user must
have in the Microsoft Windows OS in order to perform certain tasks in
the software. Refer to User Accounts.
Task Minimum Permission
Using Software Standard User
Installing Software Administrator
Upgrading Software Administrator
Setting up Users Standard User
LECO Service Standard User
Data File Permissions
To use LECO software, read-write access is required for all the data
files. The proper permissions are automatically set up during the
software installation; however, if these permissions are ever changed,
file access errors can occur. The following data folders require read-
write permissions: use the appropriate drive letter, typically
C:\ProgramData\LECO (hidden OS folder).
2–44 844 Series Installation

Antivirus Software
LECO recommends that antivirus checking be turned off for the
following files to avoid interference with data collection or storage:
use the appropriate drive letter, typically C:\ProgramData\LECO
(hidden OS folder).
Windows Settings
To use LECO software, certain Windows settings are required to ensure
performance of the instrument. These settings are set up automatically
during software installation; however, problems may be encountered if
they are ever changed. The required Windows settings are listed in the
following table.
Power Options Required Value
Sleep Sleep after Never (0 minutes)
Hard Disk Turn Off hard disk after Never (0 minutes)
Instrument Ethernet Adapter
The Ethernet adapter that connects to the instrument requires specific
settings to allow communication. For a single instrument, the adapter
must be named “LECO Hardware”. In the Properties for LECO
Hardware, Internet Protocol Version 4 (TCP/IPv4) must be enabled and
all other items disabled.
The following settings are automatically applied to the adapter during
software installation. If these settings are changed, communication
errors may occur.
For a dual-instrument configuration, refer to Dual-Instrument
Configurations, page 2–46.
Setting Value
IP Address 10.10.10.2
Subnet Mask 255.255.255.0
It is required that PCs used for running LECO applications be
configured with a separate Ethernet adapter dedicated to
communications with LECO instrumentation. (Connection to a
network would require a second Ethernet port.) If the network card
is connected to both the instrument and a LAN, communication with
the instrument will be unreliable.
Installation 844 Series 2–45

Dual-Instrument Configurations
For a dual-instrument configuration, the two connections for the
instruments must be named “LECO Hardware 1” and “LECO
Hardware 2”.
|     | Connection Name | Setting     | Value         |     |
| --- | --------------- | ----------- | ------------- | --- |
|     | LECO Hardware 1 | IP Address  | 10.10.10.3    |     |
|     | LECO Hardware 1 | Subnet Mask | 255.255.255.0 |     |
|     | LECO Hardware 2 | IP Address  | 10.10.10.4    |     |
|     | LECO Hardware 2 | Subnet Mask | 255.255.255.0 |     |
Installing the Software
1. Turn On the computer tower.
CAUTION: NETWORK CONNECTION
Connect the Ethernet port on the instrument to the
appropriate port on the computer as instructed in this manual.
Connecting the instrument directly to a corporate network
(LAN) may result in communication problems.
2. If the Ethernet cable was not previously connected, locate the
Ethernet cable and connect one end to the Ethernet adapter card
on the rear of the computer. Connect the other end to the
|     | Ethernet Port  | on the rear of the instrument. Refer to  |     |     |
| --- | -------------- | ---------------------------------------- | --- | --- |
Instrument Rear Panel Overview, page 2–10.
3. Make sure that the Ethernet adapter connected to the instrument
has been named “LECO Hardware”. For a dual-instrument
|     | configuration, refer to Dual-Instrument Configurations, |     |     | previous. |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------- | --- | --- | --------- |
4. Complete one of the following steps to acquire administration-
level permission, which is required for installation.
· Log in to the operating system using an administrator-level
account.
Or
|     | · Log in as normal | and have someone provide administrator  |     |     |
| --- | ------------------ | --------------------------------------- | --- | --- |
credentials for the install.
5. Insert the software installation media.
Or
Navigate to https://www.lecosoftware.com/cornerstone, and
select Web Installer or Offline Installer.
| 2–46 |     | 844 Series |     | Installation |
| ---- | --- | ---------- | --- | ------------ |

6. Run the installer by completing the appropriate set of steps,
depending on what was done in step 5, previous:
Software Installation Media:
A. Press Windows key + E on the keyboard to open the File
Explorer.
B. Select the desired installation media from the left-hand
navigation pane of the File Explorer.
C. Double-click “Setup” to begin the software installation.
LECO Software Website:
Run the installer downloaded in step 5, previous.
7. Follow the onscreen prompts to allow the installer to make
changes to the computer as part of the installation.
The software installation procedure may require the computer to be
restarted at the end of installation. If prompted, allow the restart.
8. At the Welcome screen, select Begin to start the installation
process.
9. At the Preparing to Install Cornerstone screen, insert the LECO-
supplied Registration key into a USB slot on the PC if not
completed previously.
10. At the Install Cornerstone screen, select Install to install the
Cornerstone brand software.
11. At the Installation Complete screen, select Finish. The icon for the
instrument software will appear on the desktop.
12. Complete the steps in Setting up Microsoft Windows for
Touchscreen Operation, page 2–51.
13. Double-click the instrument software desktop icon to
start the software.
· If a dual-instrument configuration is being used, launch the
Cornerstone brand software twice to launch two instances of the
software and allow for control of each instrument. Select the
desired instance of Cornerstone by selecting it from the Windows
taskbar.
· For a dual-instrument configuration, ensure that a Cornerstone
brand software registration key that contains configurations for
each instrument is used.
Installation 844 Series 2–47

Configuring Firewall Settings
When the LECO software is installed, it automatically configures the
Microsoft Windows firewall settings for the instrument software. (If the
Microsoft Windows firewall settings are ever changed, the easiest fix is
to reinstall the LECO software.)
If a firewall program other than Microsoft Windows is used, exclude
“C:\Program Files\LECO Cornerstone\LECO Cornerstone.exe” from all
firewall restrictions.
Troubleshooting Registration Key
· A Registration Key is required to use the software.
· The Registration Key should remain installed in one of the USB
ports while using the software.
Registration Key Error Messages
The Cornerstone Registration Key Not Present or Invalid dialog box
appears if the registration key is not installed.
1. Verify the registration key is installed.
2. Verify the registration key driver is installed.
3. Select OK, and then restart the software.
2–48 844 Series Installation

Adding Options to the Registration Key
The steps in this section should be completed only when additional
features are purchased.
The file “Cornerstone.CopyProtectionKey.v2c” can be used only once
and it cannot be used to update an instrument other than the unit
specified in step 1, following.
1. Select the Information button in the upper left corner of the
Cornerstone brand software.
2. The Information screen appears. Record the Registration ID and
Registration Date, and then select the “X” to close.
3. Provide your local LECO service representative with the product
name, serial number, Registration ID, Registered On date, and
the type of option to be added to the instrument.
4. A file named “Cornerstone.CopyProtectionKey.v2c” will be
returned to the customer. Place this file in the instrument PC’s
Documents folder (C:\Users\Customer\Documents).
5. Start the Cornerstone brand software. The software will
automatically read the file, update the options on the registration
key, and delete the file when done.
6. Any software features associated with that option will be enabled
in the Cornerstone brand software.
Uninstalling LECO Software
The following screenshot illustrations are examples only and may be
different depending on what version of Windows you are using.
The following section explains how to uninstall LECO software.
1. Select the Start button in the lower left corner.
2. Enter the following text in the Search field: add or remove
programs. When Add or Remove Programs appears in the
matches, select it.
Installation 844 Series 2–49

3. Scroll until LECO Cornerstone appears, and then select it.
4. Select Uninstall.
5. When prompted, verify that the name of the software being
uninstalled is correct, and then confirm the uninstallation.
| 2–50 | 844 Series | Installation |

Operation

2. Select Properties/Settings.

Analysis
The Analysis chapter explains how to prepare the sample for an
analysis once the instrument is set up.
HAZARDOUS VOLTAGE WARNING
During installation and operation of this instrument, the
ON/OFF switch must be easily accessible. Refer to Figure 2-4,
page 2–12, for the location of the ON/OFF switch.
PROTECTIVE EYEWEAR/PROTECTIVE GLOVES
Protective eyewear and gloves should be worn when handling
chemicals.
Refer to the Safety Data Sheet (SDS) for the specific chemical
for additional information.

Software Overview
After the Cornerstone® brand software has been started, the main
window will appear. The following section explains the items on the
main window.
Depending on your configuration, the following software overview
screen may not appear exactly as the screen on your instrument.
Some items, such as scrollbars and scroll arrows, may not appear
depending on screen size, screen orientation, and display settings.
The software automatically detects whether the monitor is in
portrait or landscape mode and runs the software to fit. Figure 3-1,
following, shows a monitor in portrait mode.
Figure 3-1
Software Overview Portrait Mode
Analysis 844 Series 3–5

Title Bar—Identifies the software. For dual-instrument configurations,
the unit number for the connected instrument will also be displayed.
Toolbar—Provides the means to quickly find and perform
specific tasks.
Selection Menu—Provides the means to quickly select the desired
items.
Toolbar More Buttons—Provides the means to scroll through the
toolbar items when there are more buttons than can display on the
screen at once.
Horizontal Scroll Arrows—Provides the means to scroll right and left
through columns when the number of columns defined in the display is
greater than can be shown on the screen.
Expand/Collapse Arrow—Provides the means to expand or collapse
additional information associated with sets and reps.
Sets/Reps—These fields list information relevant to running a sample
or the results from a sample (replicate) or set of samples (set). Sets
include information such as sample type, sample name, number of
replicates, description and method. Replicates include information such
as mass, comments, and sample results. In the software, check
standards that are out of range are indicated by orange text;
saturated cell results are indicated by blue text. Refer to Configuring
the Display, page 4–5.
Vertical Scroll Bar—Provides the means to move up and down
through the list of sample sets/reps.
Login Bar—Provides the means to log in blanks, standards, drifts,
samples, gas doses, and templates. Refer to Logging in Replicates for
Analysis, page 3–34.
Plot Settings—Provides the means to select the desired options and
configure the plot.
Resize Plots—This tab, and the arrows on either side of the screen,
can be selected and dragged to resize the plot.
Plot Layout Button—Changes whether the plots are stacked
vertically or horizontally. Available only when the instrument
configuration has two or more analytes.
Scatter/Curve/Peak Plots—Plot of the analysis results.
Gauges—Displays system status. Refer to Ambient Gauges,
page 3–15.
Expand/Collapse Gauges—These icons can be selected to hide or
show the gauges.
Navigation Buttons—Provides access to the main areas of the
software.
Utility Buttons— Provides the means to perform common tasks such
as leak check, turn On the gas and vacuum, raise or lower the
pedestal, clean the pedestal, and abort the process.
3–6 844 Series Analysis

Status Bar—Displays instrument messages.
Guide/Help Buttons—Select Guide for a software demonstration;
select Help to access the most recent instruction manual.
Analyze Button—Initiates or continues analyses.
Notification Center Bell—Refer to Notification Center, page 3–14.

Notification Center
The Notification Center relays messages to the user including the
status of tasks that take time to complete, errors, or when it is time to
perform maintenance. The Notification Center icon changes depending
on whether there are any messages.
No messages available
Messages available. The number in the red circle indicates the
number of messages available.
Complete the following steps to open Notification Center to view
messages or cancel tasks.
1. If messages are available, open Notification Center by selecting
the Notification icon . The Notification Center message
screen opens.
2. Complete the following actions as desired.
· Each message has its own action button; select the desired
button to perform that action. If necessary, the software
automatically navigates to the screen where that action can
be performed. Some common actions include:
View Instrument Maintenance—Automatically navigates in
o
the software to Instrument Ø Maintenance Ø Counters.
Ignore—Ignores a counter until it is re-evaluated and
o
removes the task from the Notification Center.
Dismiss—Removes the task from the Notification Center.
o
· Select Dismiss All to select Dismiss on all notifications at once.
· Select “X” to close the Notification Center message screen.
The Notification icon remains active as long as any unresolved
messages remain.

Performing Common Software Tasks
Starting the Software
1. Double-click the instrument software desktop icon to
start the software.
If a dual-instrument configuration is being used, launch the
Cornerstone brand software twice to launch two instances of the
software and allow for control of each instrument. Select the desired
instance of Cornerstone by selecting it from the Microsoft® Windows®
taskbar.
2. At the onscreen prompt, select the desired startup.
· To start the software using the default database, select
Default Startup, and then skip to step 4, page 3–19.
· To choose a different database, select Startup Options, and
then continue with step 3, following.
If nothing is selected within 10 seconds, the default database
automatically loads.
3–18 844 Series Analysis

3. Select the desired startup location by completing one of the
following options.
· To use the default startup location:
1) Select No next to Use Custom Location.
2) Select the box next to Startup Database and choose the
desired database.
3) Select OK.
· To choose a different startup location:
1) Select Yes next to Use Custom Location.
2) Select the box next to Location and choose the desired file
location.
3) Select Accept, and then select OK.
The selected database will now be considered the Default Startup
database the next time the software is started..
4. The software opens. If User Security is enabled, the Session
menu appears.
· The Session menu is accessible at any time by selecting the
Session button on the Title bar. When User
Security is enabled in the Administrator account, the active user
name appears. Refer to Enabling User Permissions From the
Administrator Account, page 4–60, for information on User
Security.
· The following screenshot example shows options that may not be
applicable to all instrument configurations.
Analysis 844 Series 3–19

Selecting a Language
The Select Language feature allows the operator to localize the
Cornerstone brand software for a specific language. The available
language choices appear on the screen. Although the software is
localized for a specific language, the built-in manual and help files will
remain in English.
1. Select the Session button.
2. Select the Language field.
3. Select the language desired.
4. Select Enter. The software will convert to the selected language.

Configuring the Plot
To configure a plot, select the Plot Settings button . An option
box to configure the plot appears. Clear the option box by touching the
screen anywhere outside the box.
The option box to configure the plot will vary depending on the type
of display and the instrument configuration.
· Select Curve to change the plot to a signal curve.
· Select Advanced to plot the signal curve with extra information,
such as y-axis values or an ambient trace. To view x- and y-axis
coordinates, select and hold on a trace. Refer to Configuring the
Display, page 4–5.
· Select Scatter and the results will appear as a scatter plot.
Scatter mode requires a minimum of two replicates to be
displayed.
3–22 844 Series Analysis

· To Zoom and magnify the plot, use one of the following methods.
On single-touch displays: Select Zoom. Select and hold a
o
corner of the area of interest, and then slowly drag diagonally
toward the opposite corner of the area.
On multi-touch displays:
o
§ Select Zoom. With a single finger, touch and hold a corner
of the area of interest, and then slowly drag diagonally
toward the opposite corner of the area.
§ Select Multi-Touch. Touch the plot with two fingers and
drag apart to zoom in, or pinch together to zoom out.
Either axis can be zoomed, but not simultaneously. The
axis that the user’s fingers more closely align with
determines which axis zooms. The same axis is affected
until the fingers are removed from the screen.
On all displays: Select Zoom or Multi-Touch, if available.
o
Rotate the mouse wheel forward to zoom in or backward to
zoom out. Both axes scale. To scale only one axis, hold the
Ctrl key to scale the x-axis, or hold Shift to scale the y-axis.
· Once the plot is zoomed as described in Zoom, previous, use one
of the following methods to Pan the plot left to right or up to
down.
On single-touch displays: Select Pan. Move the plot left to
o
right or up to down using the mouse cursor.
On multi-touch displays:
o
§ Select Pan. Move the plot left to right or up to down using
the mouse cursor or finger.
§ Touch the plot with two fingers and drag to move the plot
as desired.
· Select Restore to restore the plot to its default view.
· Toggle the desired analyte button Off (gray) to
minimize the plot. To restore the plot, toggle the desired analyte
button On (colored) .
· Select Advanced, and then select the Signals button to
show or hide the different areas of signal such as baseline,
integration, and endline. The button appears only when a single
replicate is selected.
Analysis 844 Series 3–23

Configuring the Calculations Plots
The calculations (calibration, drift, and blank) plots have additional
settings that can be configured by the user.
· Select All to see the entire plot.
· Select Included to focus on the data points included in the
current calculation.
· Select the desired plot type from the drop-down list. Plot Type is
available only within calibrations. The Plot Type Options are
Adjusted Area, Absolute Error, and Relative Error. When changing
the plot type, the displayed replicate data changes to match the
plot.
Importing and Exporting
All data imports use the process described in this section, except for
sample files. For information on importing sample files, refer to
Creating and Importing a Sample File, page 3–37.
Importing
Import allows the user to restore exported data to the Cornerstone
brand software.
1. Select Import . The file browser opens.
2. Navigate to the location of the file to import using the file
browser, and then select Accept.
3. Select the file(s) to import, and then select Import. The software
displays a message under the Import action button with the
name(s) of the file(s) being imported and the path name(s).
4. Select Save to complete the import process.
3–24 844 Series Analysis

Exporting
To export information from the Analysis screen, refer to the
instructions in Export Output, page 3–25.
Export allows the user to save information from the Cornerstone brand
software to an outside location.
1. Select the information to be exported.
2. Select Export . The file browser opens.
3. Navigate to the location to export the file using the file browser.
Use the Add, Rename, and Delete buttons to modify folders on
the computer.
4. Select Accept to confirm the location.
5. The software displays a message under the Export action button
with the name(s) of the file(s) being exported and the path
name(s).
Export Output
The following instructions are for exporting information from the
Analysis screen. To export information from another screen, refer to
the instructions in Exporting, page 3–25.
1. From the Analysis screen, select the set(s) to export.
2. Select Output on the Toolbar.
3. Select the Export tab, and then select the data type to be
exported. The following example uses Plot Data.
4. To choose a new export location, select the box next to Location
(or skip to step 5, following, to use the default location). A file
browser opens. Navigate to the location to export the information
using the file browser. Use the Add, Rename, and Delete buttons
to modify folders on the computer. Select Accept to continue, or
select Close to cancel any changes made to the file location and
close the File Location Selection window.
Analysis 844 Series 3–25

5. Select Export to export the selected data, or select Close to close
the output tabs and cancel the export.
6. The following files are created for the selected data type. Then,
the export is complete.
Plot Data:
· A file named “Plots.csv” is created in the selected location.
Sample Data:
· A zipped folder containing the sample data is created in the
selected location.
Sample Data with Instrument Report:
· A zipped folder containing the sample data and an Instrument
Report PDF are created in the selected location.
Including and Excluding
When an item is included, it can then be used for other tasks in the
software – such as in the login, blank, calibration, and drifting
processes – and it is used to determine results on the Analysis screen.
When an item is excluded, it remains in the database but is not
available for use in other tasks. When a counter is excluded, it is no
longer part of the instrument maintenance process.
The following symbols are used to denote excluded items, sets, and
replicates:
· All excluded items.
· On the Analysis screen, sets that contain only excluded
replicates.
· On the Analysis screen, sets that contain a combination of
excluded and included replicates.
Cloning
1. Select the information to be cloned.
2. Select Clone . The cloned item appears in the list as
“X Copy,” where “X” is the name of the item that was copied.
3–26 844 Series Analysis

The following procedure explains how to personalize the software’s
Analyze button with a company logo.
1. Shut down the Cornerstone brand software.
2. Name or rename the image file “CustomLogo.png.” or
“CustomLogo.jpg.” The file must be a .png or .jpg image to work
properly.
3. Place the custom logo .png or .jpg file here:
C:\ProgramData\LECO\CustomLogo.png
Or
C:\ProgramData\LECO\CustomLogo.jpg
The file name must match exactly.
4. Restart the Cornerstone brand software.

Using the Selection Menu
The Selection menu is available from the Analysis screen. The

Select Multi again to return to Single-Item Select mode.

A. Select the first set/rep. This will define the beginning of the

B. Select Range.
C. Select the last set/rep. All items from the first to the last will

Analysis 844 Series 3–29

Using Select All
Select All is a feature available from the Selection menu that will select
all the fields available on the current screen. Using Select All in
conjunction with filters is convenient for selecting sets for reports and
transports.
After Select All is used, the Multi button becomes activated. Deselect
Multi to return to Single-Item Select mode. Select Clear to deselect all
items.
Using Multi Field
Multi Field is a feature available from the Selection menu that will edit
text fields from multiple sets or replicates at once if the same edit is
desired. For example, the same description can be added to multiple
sets.
1. Select Multi Field.
2. Edit the desired fields by completing one of the following
methods.
· Select one of the fields to be edited. A keyboard appears.
Select the same field in each of the other sets or replicates to
be edited. Enter the desired information, and then select
Enter.
· Select one of the fields to be edited. A keyboard appears.
Enter the desired information, and then select the same field
in each of the other sets or replicates to be edited. When all of
the desired sets or replicates have been edited, select Enter.

Daily Startup Procedure
The following section explains tasks that should be completed on a
daily basis.
| Some steps apply |     | only to some instrument configurations. |     |     |     |     |
| ---------------- | --- | --------------------------------------- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
1. Check the air filters for excess dirt. Refer to Performing Air Filter
Maintenance, page 6–59.
2. Check the flow scrubber and replace when the Anhydrone has
|     | caked. Refer to Replacing the Flow Scrubber Tube, page |     |     |     | 6–22. |     |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------ | --- | --- | --- | ----- | --- |
3. Check the particle filter and replace when the filter has darkened
|     | significantly. Refer to |     | Replacing the Particle Filter, page |     | 6–30. |     |
| --- | ----------------------- | --- | ----------------------------------- | --- | ----- | --- |
4. Inspect the incoming scrubber and replace when the Anhydrone
|     | has caked | or when the LECOSORB®                 |     | has turned | color. Refer to |     |
| --- | --------- | ------------------------------------- | --- | ---------- | --------------- | --- |
|     | Replacing | the Incoming Scrubber Tube, page 6–9. |     |            |                 |     |
5. Check the cellulose trap and replace when 1-inch of the cellulose
|     | has turned | black or if the cellulose has turned |     |     | brown. Refer to  |     |
| --- | ---------- | ------------------------------------ | --- | --- | ---------------- | --- |
Replacing the Cellulose Trap Tube, page 6–20.
6. Check Counters to ensure that no other maintenance items will
|     | expire during the planned set of analyses. Refer to |     |     |     | Counters,  |     |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------- | --- | --- | --- | ---------- | --- |
page 5–34.
| 7.  | Perform regular maintenance on the auto cleaner |                                             |     |     | or manual  |     |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------- | --- | --- | ---------- | --- |
|     | cleaner. Refer to                               | Maintaining the Auto Cleaner, page 6–31, or |     |     |            |     |
Maintaining the Manual Cleaner, page 6–34.
8. Check the dust filter and replace when it is visibly dirty. Refer to
Maintaining the Dust Filter, page 6–38.
9. Check the combustion tube and replace when there is excessive
buildup on the walls or if cracks have developed. Grease all
combustion tube o-rings. Refer to Maintaining the Combustion
Tube, page 6–35, and Cleaning and Greasing the O-Rings,
page 6–8.
10. Check the vacuum filters and manifold. Replace filters when full
and wipe manifold to remove excess dust. Refer to Maintaining
|     | the Vacuum Cleaner, page |                                             | 6–51. |     |     |     |
| --- | ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------- | ----- | --- | --- | --- |
| 11. | Check the                | crucible bucket, and empty it if necessary. |       |     |     |     |
12. Perform and pass a leak check. Refer to Leak Check, page 8–17.
13. Turn On the gas and verify that “Ready to Analyze” appears in the
status bar.
14. If QC is enabled, perform necessary blanks, checks and/or
|     | calibration in order to be in control. Refer to Configuring |     |     |     | Quality  |     |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------- | --- | --- | --- | -------- | --- |
Control, page 4–21.
| Analysis |     |     | 844 Series |     |     | 3–31 |
| -------- | --- | --- | ---------- | --- | --- | ---- |

Crucibles
When analyzing low carbon (<0.1%) or low sulfur (<0.01%) samples,
pre-baking the crucibles is necessary to achieve the best results. The
crucibles are “burned off” by firing them at 1250 °C for at least
15 minutes, or at 1000 °C for at least 40 minutes, in a TF2, TF4, or
TF10 LECO Tube Furnace. Loading and unloading the crucibles into the
tube furnace is crucial to ensuring they are sufficiently prepared for
low-composition analyses.
· Temperatures up to 1350 °C may be acceptable, depending
on the furnace being used.
· Prior to loading the crucibles, verify that the tube furnace is
regulated at the desired temperature.
The following steps describe how to prepare crucibles using one side of
the tube furnace. The same procedure can be repeated with additional
tube(s), if applicable.
1. For an empty tube, feed 14 crucibles, positioned on their sides,
into the furnace. The first set of seven crucibles are positioned in
the “hot zone” of the furnace.
2. After the crucibles have baked for the minimum time based on
the set temperature, insert another seven crucibles into the back
of the furnace. The new crucibles will push the first set of seven
crucibles into a cooler portion of the furnace and the middle set of
seven crucibles into the “hot zone” of the furnace. A total of 21
crucibles should now be loaded in the furnace.
3. Again, wait the minimum amount of time based on the
temperature setpoint for the first set of seven crucibles to cool
and the middle set of seven to bake. Remove the first set of
seven crucibles from the furnace and place them on a heat-
resistant surface.
HIGH TEMPERATURE WARNING
Though the removed crucibles have been “cooling” in a cooler
portion of the tube furnace, they are still hot (up to 600 °C).
Do not touch the crucibles with bare hands. Use only crucible
tongs to transfer the crucibles. The last two crucibles removed
from the furnace may still be too hot to place directly on a
crucible tray. They can be allowed to cool in the front of the
furnace for approximately 60 to 90 seconds prior to removal.
4. Transfer the crucibles to a desiccator for storage if they will not
be immediately used.
3–32 844 Series Analysis

5. Repeat this process of loading and unloading sets of seven
crucibles as needed to ensure the crucibles are sufficiently pre-
baked and subsequently cooled.
CAUTION
Do not touch the crucibles with bare hands after they have
been pre-baked in the tube furnace, even when they are cool.
Natural oils from your hands will contaminate the clean
crucibles.
If the pre-baked crucibles are not used within 4 hours of being
removed from the tube furnace, they must be pre-baked again.
Analysis 844 Series 3–33

· Gas Dose—Select the gas dose standard to use in the
analysis, and then select Enter. All gas doses automatically
have a mass determined by the Nominal Mass field set in the
Method. This value can be edited only from the Method Edit
screen. Refer to Creating and Editing Standards, page 4–48.
4. For standard, drift, and sample only: For each rep, enter the
mass of the replicate, select Default: [X], select Read Mass from
Balance, or press the Print button on the balance, and then select
Enter.
5. Select Method in the set row, and then select a method name
from the list.
Logging in a Template
Sample templates are a quick way of logging in multiple sample sets
that comprise a routine analysis pattern (for example: running five
blanks, two check standards, and three normal unknown samples each
day). For more information on templates, refer to Creating and Editing
Templates, page 4–50, and Using Sample Templates, page 4–51.
Splitting
A replicate can be split off from a set and made into a new set of its
own. The relevant Set fields (for example: Name) will be copied into
the new set. The comments associated with each sample replicate will
be retained.
1. Select the replicate(s) to be split.
2. Select Split from the Toolbar on the Analysis screen.
Analysis 844 Series 3–35

Merging
To merge two or more sets into one, the Sample Type, Name, and
Method, must match. The Description and Comment fields do not have
to match. When sets are merged, sample replicate comments are
retained. The merged sample sets will have the same description as is
associated with the first sample set.
Depending on the instrument configuration, additional set
parameters may need to match in order to merge two or more sets.
1. Select the sets to be merged.
2. Select Merge from the Toolbar on the Analysis screen.
Excluding or Deleting a Sample
A sample can be excluded from the set result or permanently deleted
from the software.
A standard sample that is referenced by a calibration, drift, or blank
cannot be deleted.
1. Select the sample set or sample rep to exclude or delete.
2. Select Exclude . Once a rep has been excluded, its
results will not be used in the set results or when setting a blank,
drift, or calibration.
3. To permanently delete a sample, select the sample again and
select Delete .
Or
To re-include the sample, select the sample again and then select
Include .
Any unanalyzed sample with a mass, except for blanks, must be
excluded before it can be deleted.
3–36 844 Series Analysis

Creating and Importing a Sample File
Sample Import allows the user to import sample files (in .csv format)
|     | into the Cornerstone | brand software to analyze. To transmit the  |     |

results back to the .csv file, refer to Data Transmit, page 5–40.
